Surah: 53. Follow the brief below (surah_map.md) exactly. The evidence is text.md (the surah), dictionary.md (every root of the surah's words, each branch with the classical dictionaries' own phrases), channels.md and hft.md (both are earlier readers' proposals: ignore their judgements (grades, strength or confidence labels, reading types, statements of what a reading may or may not do) and make your own) and your own knowledge of Arabic and the Quran. Return only the map as your final message.

===== _commentary/v16/prompts/r2/surah_map.md =====
Read the surah as one text and write its map of image chains: the lexical images that run through several of
its ayat and join them into one scene, process or movement. A later writer will read one ayah at a time, with
only that ayah's own dictionary. The map lets that writer hear what the ayah's words carry in the surah as a
whole, including senses whose evidence sits under the words of other ayat.

Your evidence is the surah text, the dictionary of every root in the surah (each branch with the classical
dictionaries' own phrases), earlier readers' channel review (channels.md) and activation hypotheses (hft.md),
and your own knowledge of Arabic and the Quran. Where a member or passage comes from memory rather than from
the dictionary or the text, say so. Do not use tools, delegate, browse or inspect files.

The channel review and the HFT records are proposals by earlier readers. Ignore their judgements: grades,
strength or confidence labels, reading types, words such as "surprising", "exploratory" or "latent", and every
statement of what a reading may or may not do. Make your own judgement from the surah's words, the dictionary
phrases and the Quran. Do not rediscover what they already assembled: start from their chains, test each one
against the dictionary phrases and the text, and join, extend, split or correct them. Where their wording
abstracts a member, go back to the dictionary phrase and name what it actually says.

A chain belongs on the map when its members are senses the dictionary attests for words that stand in the
surah, and together they make one image or process that the surah's wording or sequence lets a listener hear.
A member need not be the sense that translates its word in its own ayah; a chain is heard across the surah,
not in one word. Say plainly when a member is a rare sense or is attested only inside a fixed expression, and
keep it. When the dictionary itself joins two of the surah's words in one phrase, quote that phrase: it is
the strongest evidence a chain can have. Keep a scene at the level of its objects, their parts and their
operation. When proposals share members, do not fold one into another's more abstract function unless nothing
concrete is lost. Carry every chain that meets this test, however unusual; leave out proposals that do not.

Write the map in English, with Arabic quoted exactly (surah wording from the text, dictionary phrases from the
dictionary). It is working material for the writer, not commentary prose.

1. `## Chains`. For each chain, a `###` heading naming the image. One paragraph on what the image is and how it
   moves through the surah. Then its members, one line each: the ayah ref, the word as it stands in the text,
   the root and branch id, the dictionary's own phrase quoted exactly, and what this member contributes to the
   image. Add Quran passages outside the surah, with exact refs, where they stage or confirm the chain.
2. `## Ayat`. For each ayah in order: which chains it opens, advances or completes, and what its words add to
   each.
3. `## Not carried`. Each channel subchannel and each HFT record you did not carry into a chain, one line each:
   its name and why.

No ranking and no labels of strength or confidence. No list of what a writer must include. There is no length
target and no required number of chains or members.


===== _commentary/v16/work/s053/surah.r2/text.md =====
# Surah 53

- 53:1 وَٱلنَّجْمِ إِذَا هَوَىٰ
- 53:2 مَا ضَلَّ صَاحِبُكُمْ وَمَا غَوَىٰ
- 53:3 وَمَا يَنطِقُ عَنِ ٱلْهَوَىٰٓ
- 53:4 إِنْ هُوَ إِلَّا وَحْىٌۭ يُوحَىٰ
- 53:5 عَلَّمَهُۥ شَدِيدُ ٱلْقُوَىٰ
- 53:6 ذُو مِرَّةٍۢ فَٱسْتَوَىٰ
- 53:7 وَهُوَ بِٱلْأُفُقِ ٱلْأَعْلَىٰ
- 53:8 ثُمَّ دَنَا فَتَدَلَّىٰ
- 53:9 فَكَانَ قَابَ قَوْسَيْنِ أَوْ أَدْنَىٰ
- 53:10 فَأَوْحَىٰٓ إِلَىٰ عَبْدِهِۦ مَآ أَوْحَىٰ
- 53:11 مَا كَذَبَ ٱلْفُؤَادُ مَا رَأَىٰٓ
- 53:12 أَفَتُمَٰرُونَهُۥ عَلَىٰ مَا يَرَىٰ
- 53:13 وَلَقَدْ رَءَاهُ نَزْلَةً أُخْرَىٰ
- 53:14 عِندَ سِدْرَةِ ٱلْمُنتَهَىٰ
- 53:15 عِندَهَا جَنَّةُ ٱلْمَأْوَىٰٓ
- 53:16 إِذْ يَغْشَى ٱلسِّدْرَةَ مَا يَغْشَىٰ
- 53:17 مَا زَاغَ ٱلْبَصَرُ وَمَا طَغَىٰ
- 53:18 لَقَدْ رَأَىٰ مِنْ ءَايَٰتِ رَبِّهِ ٱلْكُبْرَىٰٓ
- 53:19 أَفَرَءَيْتُمُ ٱللَّٰتَ وَٱلْعُزَّىٰ
- 53:20 وَمَنَوٰةَ ٱلثَّالِثَةَ ٱلْأُخْرَىٰٓ
- 53:21 أَلَكُمُ ٱلذَّكَرُ وَلَهُ ٱلْأُنثَىٰ
- 53:22 تِلْكَ إِذًۭا قِسْمَةٌۭ ضِيزَىٰٓ
- 53:23 إِنْ هِىَ إِلَّآ أَسْمَآءٌۭ سَمَّيْتُمُوهَآ أَنتُمْ وَءَابَآؤُكُم مَّآ أَنزَلَ ٱللَّهُ بِهَا مِن سُلْطَٰنٍ ۚ إِن يَتَّبِعُونَ إِلَّا ٱلظَّنَّ وَمَا تَهْوَى ٱلْأَنفُسُ ۖ وَلَقَدْ جَآءَهُم مِّن رَّبِّهِمُ ٱلْهُدَىٰٓ
- 53:24 أَمْ لِلْإِنسَٰنِ مَا تَمَنَّىٰ
- 53:25 فَلِلَّهِ ٱلْءَاخِرَةُ وَٱلْأُولَىٰ
- 53:26 ۞ وَكَم مِّن مَّلَكٍۢ فِى ٱلسَّمَٰوَٰتِ لَا تُغْنِى شَفَٰعَتُهُمْ شَيْـًٔا إِلَّا مِنۢ بَعْدِ أَن يَأْذَنَ ٱللَّهُ لِمَن يَشَآءُ وَيَرْضَىٰٓ
- 53:27 إِنَّ ٱلَّذِينَ لَا يُؤْمِنُونَ بِٱلْءَاخِرَةِ لَيُسَمُّونَ ٱلْمَلَٰٓئِكَةَ تَسْمِيَةَ ٱلْأُنثَىٰ
- 53:28 وَمَا لَهُم بِهِۦ مِنْ عِلْمٍ ۖ إِن يَتَّبِعُونَ إِلَّا ٱلظَّنَّ ۖ وَإِنَّ ٱلظَّنَّ لَا يُغْنِى مِنَ ٱلْحَقِّ شَيْـًۭٔا
- 53:29 فَأَعْرِضْ عَن مَّن تَوَلَّىٰ عَن ذِكْرِنَا وَلَمْ يُرِدْ إِلَّا ٱلْحَيَوٰةَ ٱلدُّنْيَا
- 53:30 ذَٰلِكَ مَبْلَغُهُم مِّنَ ٱلْعِلْمِ ۚ إِنَّ رَبَّكَ هُوَ أَعْلَمُ بِمَن ضَلَّ عَن سَبِيلِهِۦ وَهُوَ أَعْلَمُ بِمَنِ ٱهْتَدَىٰ
- 53:31 وَلِلَّهِ مَا فِى ٱلسَّمَٰوَٰتِ وَمَا فِى ٱلْأَرْضِ لِيَجْزِىَ ٱلَّذِينَ أَسَٰٓـُٔوا۟ بِمَا عَمِلُوا۟ وَيَجْزِىَ ٱلَّذِينَ أَحْسَنُوا۟ بِٱلْحُسْنَى
- 53:32 ٱلَّذِينَ يَجْتَنِبُونَ كَبَٰٓئِرَ ٱلْإِثْمِ وَٱلْفَوَٰحِشَ إِلَّا ٱللَّمَمَ ۚ إِنَّ رَبَّكَ وَٰسِعُ ٱلْمَغْفِرَةِ ۚ هُوَ أَعْلَمُ بِكُمْ إِذْ أَنشَأَكُم مِّنَ ٱلْأَرْضِ وَإِذْ أَنتُمْ أَجِنَّةٌۭ فِى بُطُونِ أُمَّهَٰتِكُمْ ۖ فَلَا تُزَكُّوٓا۟ أَنفُسَكُمْ ۖ هُوَ أَعْلَمُ بِمَنِ ٱتَّقَىٰٓ
- 53:33 أَفَرَءَيْتَ ٱلَّذِى تَوَلَّىٰ
- 53:34 وَأَعْطَىٰ قَلِيلًۭا وَأَكْدَىٰٓ
- 53:35 أَعِندَهُۥ عِلْمُ ٱلْغَيْبِ فَهُوَ يَرَىٰٓ
- 53:36 أَمْ لَمْ يُنَبَّأْ بِمَا فِى صُحُفِ مُوسَىٰ
- 53:37 وَإِبْرَٰهِيمَ ٱلَّذِى وَفَّىٰٓ
- 53:38 أَلَّا تَزِرُ وَازِرَةٌۭ وِزْرَ أُخْرَىٰ
- 53:39 وَأَن لَّيْسَ لِلْإِنسَٰنِ إِلَّا مَا سَعَىٰ
- 53:40 وَأَنَّ سَعْيَهُۥ سَوْفَ يُرَىٰ
- 53:41 ثُمَّ يُجْزَىٰهُ ٱلْجَزَآءَ ٱلْأَوْفَىٰ
- 53:42 وَأَنَّ إِلَىٰ رَبِّكَ ٱلْمُنتَهَىٰ
- 53:43 وَأَنَّهُۥ هُوَ أَضْحَكَ وَأَبْكَىٰ
- 53:44 وَأَنَّهُۥ هُوَ أَمَاتَ وَأَحْيَا
- 53:45 وَأَنَّهُۥ خَلَقَ ٱلزَّوْجَيْنِ ٱلذَّكَرَ وَٱلْأُنثَىٰ
- 53:46 مِن نُّطْفَةٍ إِذَا تُمْنَىٰ
- 53:47 وَأَنَّ عَلَيْهِ ٱلنَّشْأَةَ ٱلْأُخْرَىٰ
- 53:48 وَأَنَّهُۥ هُوَ أَغْنَىٰ وَأَقْنَىٰ
- 53:49 وَأَنَّهُۥ هُوَ رَبُّ ٱلشِّعْرَىٰ
- 53:50 وَأَنَّهُۥٓ أَهْلَكَ عَادًا ٱلْأُولَىٰ
- 53:51 وَثَمُودَا۟ فَمَآ أَبْقَىٰ
- 53:52 وَقَوْمَ نُوحٍۢ مِّن قَبْلُ ۖ إِنَّهُمْ كَانُوا۟ هُمْ أَظْلَمَ وَأَطْغَىٰ
- 53:53 وَٱلْمُؤْتَفِكَةَ أَهْوَىٰ
- 53:54 فَغَشَّىٰهَا مَا غَشَّىٰ
- 53:55 فَبِأَىِّ ءَالَآءِ رَبِّكَ تَتَمَارَىٰ
- 53:56 هَٰذَا نَذِيرٌۭ مِّنَ ٱلنُّذُرِ ٱلْأُولَىٰٓ
- 53:57 أَزِفَتِ ٱلْءَازِفَةُ
- 53:58 لَيْسَ لَهَا مِن دُونِ ٱللَّهِ كَاشِفَةٌ
- 53:59 أَفَمِنْ هَٰذَا ٱلْحَدِيثِ تَعْجَبُونَ
- 53:60 وَتَضْحَكُونَ وَلَا تَبْكُونَ
- 53:61 وَأَنتُمْ سَٰمِدُونَ
- 53:62 فَٱسْجُدُوا۟ لِلَّهِ وَٱعْبُدُوا۟ ۩


===== _commentary/v16/work/s053/surah.r2/dictionary.md =====
# Dictionary: every branch of every focus root

Every root of the surah's words, each once, headed by the ayah and word where it first occurs; it also serves the later words listed with it.
One entry per branch: a Turkish label and Turkish glosses of its attested senses, then the classical dictionaries' own phrases with their source tags (ayn, sihah, tahdhib, maqayis, mufradat, jamhara). [kalıp] marks a sense the dictionaries attest only inside a fixed expression.
Identity roots come from the quran-data gateway. Word-scoped alternatives are cited analyses of this
exact word. **Echo roots** are observed but withheld mappings: sound-family candidates, not identity.

## ن ج م (root_001475): 53:1 وَٱلنَّجْمِ

- **B001** yıldız veya gökte görünen ışıklı cisim — yıldız; bazı bağlamlarda belirli bir yıldız kümesi · Ay'ın konaklarından biri · yıldızların bütünü
  النجم الثريا (maqayis;tahdhib)؛ كل كوكب يسمى نجما (ayn)؛ النجم واحد النجوم (jamhara)؛ النجم الكوكب (sihah)؛ النجوم تجمع الكواكب كلها (tahdhib)؛ أصل النجم الكوكب الطالع (mufradat)
- **B002** yükselip ortaya çıkmak; başlangıcı bulunmak — yükselip ortaya çıkmak, görünür olmak · bu işin bir başlangıcı ya da dayanağı yok · yükselip beliren, görünür olan · ortaya çıkan topluluk veya olay
  أصل صحيح يدل على طلوع وظهور (maqayis)؛ نجم النجم طلع (maqayis)؛ كل طالع ناجم (jamhara)؛ نجم الشيء ظهر وطلع (sihah)؛ نجم السن والقرن والنبت ونجم الخارجي (sihah)؛ يقال لكل ما طلع قد نجم (tahdhib)؛ ليس لهذا الأمر نجم أي أصل (tahdhib)
- **B003** gövdesiz, yerde yayılarak büyüyen bitki — gövdesiz, yerde yayılarak büyüyen bitki · ilkbaharda köklerden beliren sürgünler · yere yayılan küçük bitki
  النجم من النبات ما لم يكن له ساق (maqayis;sihah)؛ النجم من النبات ما لم يقم على ساق (ayn)؛ ما نجم من البقل على غير ساق (jamhara)؛ النجوم ما نجم من العروق أيام الربيع (ayn;tahdhib)؛ النجمة نبتة صغيرة (tahdhib)؛ النجمة تنبت ممتدة على وجه الأرض (tahdhib)
- **B004** belirli zamanda gelen taksit veya aşamalı bölüm — kutsal kitabın bölüm bölüm indirilen parçaları · taksit veya belirlenmiş ödeme zamanı · borcu belirli vadelere ve taksitlere bölmek
  النجوم وظائف الأشياء وكل وظيفة نجم (ayn;tahdhib)؛ نجوم القرآن أنزل جملة ثم أنزل نجوما (ayn)؛ الوقت الذي يحل فيه الدين (jamhara)؛ نجمت الدين تنجيما (jamhara)؛ نجمت المال إذا أديته نجوما (sihah)؛ نزول القرآن نجما بعد نجم (tahdhib)؛ الديون المنجمة (tahdhib)
- **B005** yıldızları gözlemek; bir işi düşünüp tasarlamak — bir işi nasıl yürüteceğini düşünüp tartmak · yıldızları gözleyen kişi · yıldızları gözlemek; uykusuzca onları izlemek
  نظر النجوم (ayn)؛ المنجم الذي ينظر في النجوم (ayn)؛ تنجم الرجل إذا نظر في النجوم (jamhara)؛ تنجم إذا رعى النجوم من سهر (jamhara)؛ نظر في النجوم أي تفكر ليدبر حجة (tahdhib)؛ يقال للإنسان إذا تفكر في أمر لينظر كيف يدبره نظر في النجوم (tahdhib)
- **B006** özel adlandırma kümesi — terazinin dilini taşıyan enine demir parça · atın art ayağındaki iki çıkıntılı kemik · açık seçik yol · kişinin iki topuğu · günün doğup belirdiği yer veya an
  المنجم في الميزان الحديدة المعترضة التي فيها اللسان (maqayis;sihah)؛ منجما الفرس العظمان الناتئان دوين العرقوب (jamhara)؛ المنجم الطريق الواضح (tahdhib)؛ منجما الرجل كعباها (tahdhib)؛ المنجم منجم النهار حين ينجم (tahdhib)
- **B007** göğün açılması veya yağmurun ya da soğuğun kesilmesi [kalıp] — gökyüzünün açılması ve yıldızların görünmesi · yağmurun ya da soğuğun kesilmesi
  أنجمت السماء بدت نجومها (ayn)؛ أنجمت السماء أقشعت (sihah)؛ أنجم البرد وأنجم المطر أقلع (sihah)؛ أنجم المطر إذا أقلع (tahdhib)
- **B008** kötülük ve sapkınlığın kaynağı olan kimse [kalıp] — kötülük ve sapkınlığın çıktığı kaynak kişi
  فلان منجم الباطل والضلالة أي معدنه (sihah)

## ه و ي (root_001609): 53:1 هَوَىٰ, 53:3 ٱلْهَوَىٰٓ, 53:23 تَهْوَى, 53:53 أَهْوَىٰ

- **B001** hava ve boşluk; kalpte boşluk ve yüreksizlik — hava; boşluk veya aralık · kalpleri bomboş, kavrayışsız ve kararsızdır · yüreksiz, korkak ya da akılsız kimse
  الهَواء ممدود هو الجو (ayn)؛ قلبه هَواء (ayn)؛ هَواء الجو ممدود (jamhara)؛ الهَواء ما بين السماء والأرض وكل خال هواء (sihah)؛ الهَواء والخواء واحد (tahdhib)؛ الهَواء كل فرجة بين شيئين (tahdhib)؛ هوى صدره أي خلا (tahdhib)؛ الهوهاءة الضعيف الفؤاد الجبان (tahdhib)؛ الهَواء ما بين الأرض والسماء (mufradat)؛ أصل صحيح يدل على خلو وسقوط (maqayis)؛ أصله الهَواء بين الأرض والسماء سمي لخلوه (maqayis)
- **B002** yukarıdan düşme; yönlü gidiş, derin çukur, düşürme, ölüm ve yas bağlantıları — yukarıdan aşağı düştü veya indi · dipsiz uçurum; ateş azabının adı · derin çukur veya düşme yeri · topluluk derin çukura birbiri ardınca düştü

(source: latent_activation/network/v3/reviews/s053/reader_a_pilot.md)

# s053 Semantic Channel Discovery

## Parent Channels

### 1. Calibrated Course and Bounded Sight (P1: 53:1-18)
- Semantic invariant: Reliable arrival depends on markers, measured approach, and perception that neither drifts nor exceeds its bound.
- Surface relation: direct; 53:1-2, 53:7-9, 53:14, 53:17-18.
- Surprising reach: The celestial oath and visual encounter become a navigation-and-calibration scene rather than a collection of isolated signs.

#### Subchannel A. Celestial markers establish a traversable course
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: A star rises or appears against a distant horizon, where it functions as a conspicuous landmark by which a route can be examined and known.
- Active motifs: star as celestial body `quranic:root_001475:B001/m01`; star appearing `quranic:root_001475:B002/m01`; examining stars to plan `quranic:root_001475:B005/m01`; prominent route marker `quranic:root_001475:B006/m01`; distant horizon `quranic:root_000040:B001/m01`; knowledge that uncovers a thing `quranic:root_001040:B001/m01`; landmark or road marker `quranic:root_001040:B002/m01`; visible sign `quranic:root_000074:B003/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: 53:1 (ن ج م); 53:5 (ع ل م); 53:7 (ء ف ق); 53:18 (ء ي ي).
- Synthesis: The opening star is activated as an orienting instrument: its visibility at the horizon makes a course legible and prepares for the later claim that a major sign was actually seen.

#### Subchannel B. Nearness is measured while sight holds its line
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: An approach is gauged by a bow-shaped span, brought into alignment, and stopped at an endpoint while the eye remains centered.
- Active motifs: arc or bend `quranic:root_001267:B004/m01`; nearness `quranic:root_001267:B006/m01`; measuring by an exemplar `quranic:root_001269:B001/m01`; bow or cubit measure `quranic:root_001269:B002/m01`; bow as instrument `quranic:root_001269:B003/m01`; ordinary nearness `quranic:root_000493:B001/m01`; straightening and completion `quranic:root_000766:B002/m01`; terminal point `quranic:root_001560:B002/m01`; ocular sight `quranic:root_000121:B001/m01`; eye drift `quranic:root_000658:B002/m01`; overstepping a limit `quranic:root_000937:B001/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: 53:6 (س و ي); 53:8-9 (د ن و); 53:9 (ق و ب, ق و س); 53:14 (ن ه ي); 53:17 (ب ص ر, ز ي غ, ط غ ي).
- Synthesis: The bow measure is not merely a distance comparison. It materializes a controlled approach whose validity depends equally on geometric limit and disciplined vision.

#### Subchannel C. Losing the course ends in vacancy or a pit
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A traveler loses a marker or trace, deviates from the route, and drops into an empty depression where forward movement fails.
- Active motifs: path error `quranic:root_000913:B001/m01`; lost object or trace `quranic:root_000913:B003/m01`; misguidance `quranic:root_001116:B001/m01`; hunter's pit `quranic:root_001116:B004/m01`; void or open air `quranic:root_001609:B001/m01`; falling into an abyss `quranic:root_001609:B002/m01`; barren terrain `quranic:root_001274:B003/m01`; hole or depression `quranic:root_001267:B001/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: 53:1, 53:3 (ه و ي); 53:2 (ض ل ل, غ و ي); 53:5 (ق و ي); 53:9 (ق و ب).
- Synthesis: The denial of deviation is pressured by its latent physical contrary: failed orientation is a fall from a marked course into an empty, unproductive trap.

### 2. Descent into a Receiving Place (P1: 53:1-18)
- Semantic invariant: Downward movement becomes meaningful when it is received, drawn, and settled at a bounded destination.
- Surface relation: direct; 53:1, 53:7-10, 53:13-15.
- Surprising reach: The descent vocabulary coheres with wells, buckets, lodging, and refuge, giving the encounter a receiving-place structure.

#### Subchannel A. Descent crosses upper space toward a lower station
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: Something moves from an elevated horizon, comes down in stages, and approaches a designated station.
- Active motifs: downward fall `quranic:root_001609:B003/m01`; rapid downward movement `quranic:root_001609:B008/m01`; elevation `quranic:root_001042:B001/m01`; upper direction `quranic:root_001042:B005/m01`; descent and settling `quranic:root_001492:B001/m01`; a single descent `quranic:root_001492:B010/m01`; distant horizon `quranic:root_000040:B001/m01`; nearness `quranic:root_000493:B001/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: 53:1, 53:3 (ه و ي); 53:7 (ع ل و, ء ف ق); 53:8-9 (د ن و); 53:13 (ن ز ل).
- Synthesis: The movement from horizon to nearness can be read as a staged descent whose completion requires a lower station rather than an unbounded fall.

#### Subchannel B. A bucket draws the descending object to a terminal pool
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A bucket is lowered, reaches its target through an intermediary, and draws toward a bounded body of water.
- Active motifs: bucket `quranic:root_000485:B001/m01`; well `quranic:root_000485:B001/m02`; lowering from a height `quranic:root_000485:B010/m01`; reaching a goal through an intermediary `quranic:root_000485:B004/m01`; hole or depression `quranic:root_001267:B001/m01`; terminal pool `quranic:root_001560:B004/m01`; descent and settling `quranic:root_001492:B001/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: 53:8 (د ل و); 53:9 (ق و ب); 53:13 (ن ز ل); 53:14 (ن ه ي).
- Synthesis: The lexical network turns proximity into a drawing operation: descent is controlled by an intermediary and completed at a contained endpoint.

#### Subchannel C. The destination is lodging, refuge, and hidden enclosure
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: Arrival culminates in a station that lodges a guest, gathers what approaches, and protects it within a concealed enclosure.
- Active motifs: lodging or station `quranic:root_001492:B003/m01`; guest provision `quranic:root_001492:B005/m01`; gathering as refuge `quranic:root_000070:B001/m01`; compassion in sheltering `quranic:root_000070:B002/m01`; garden hidden by trees `quranic:root_000266:B003/m01`; hiding place `quranic:root_000266:B017/m01`; nearness or presence `quranic:root_001052:B004/m01`; protective nurture `quranic:root_000532:B002/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: 53:13 (ن ز ل); 53:14-15 (ع ن د); 53:15 (ج ن ن, ء و ي); 53:18 (ر ب ب).
- Synthesis: The terminal scene is not only spatial. It combines station, hospitality, refuge, and concealment, making the boundary a protected place of reception.

### 3. Controlled Disclosure Across Veils (P1: 53:1-18)
- Semantic invariant: A hidden communication becomes warranted through ordered transmission, inward registration, and sight that withstands obstruction.
- Surface relation: direct; 53:3-5, 53:10-12, 53:16-18.
- Surprising reach: Revelation, witnessing, and visual restraint form one disclosure mechanism with distinct transmission and verification stages.

#### Subchannel A. A concealed signal is encoded and rapidly transmitted
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: Knowledge passes by hidden conveyance, gesture, inscription, or swift signal before taking a speakable form.
- Active motifs: hidden conveyance of knowledge `quranic:root_001633:B001/m01`; communicative gesture `quranic:root_001633:B002/m01`; writing or engraving `quranic:root_001633:B003/m01`; divine inspiration `quranic:root_001633:B004/m01`; speed of transmission `quranic:root_001633:B006/m01`; speaking sign `quranic:root_001519:B002/m01`; instruction `quranic:root_001040:B001/m02`; submission in service `quranic:root_000973:B003/m02`.
- Ayah anchors: 53:3 (ن ط ق); 53:4, 53:10 (و ح ي); 53:5 (ع ل م); 53:10 (ع ب د).
- Synthesis: The utterance is the endpoint of a prior hidden operation: a signal is conveyed, encoded, taught, and received under a relation of service.

#### Subchannel B. Outer sight and the inner heart register the disclosure
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: Ocular seeing supplies an appearance while the heart supplies an inward act of recognition or judgment.
- Active motifs: ocular seeing `quranic:root_000531:B001/m01`; insight through seeing `quranic:root_000531:B001/m02`; heart's judgment or thought `quranic:root_000531:B002/m01`; showing or making visible `quranic:root_000531:B012/m01`; heart as an inner flame `quranic:root_001122:B002/m01`; accusation of falsehood `quranic:root_001290:B002/m01`; ocular sight `quranic:root_000121:B001/m01`; heart-insight `quranic:root_000121:B002/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: 53:11-13, 53:18 (ر ء ي); 53:11 (ف ء د, ك ذ ب); 53:17 (ب ص ر).
- Synthesis: The heart does not replace the eye, nor the eye the heart. Their paired registration explains why the passage can distinguish seeing, inward confirmation, and denial.

#### Subchannel C. Covering pressures vision without dislodging it
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: A sweeping cover, haze, or hanging screen passes over the field of sight, yet the observer neither drifts nor exceeds the visual boundary.
- Active motifs: physical covering `quranic:root_001088:B001/m01`; sweeping universal cover `quranic:root_001088:B002/m01`; visual haze `quranic:root_000688:B003/m01`; hanging cloth `quranic:root_000688:B004/m01`; hanging hair `quranic:root_000688:B004/m02`; eye drift `quranic:root_000658:B002/m01`; shield before the eye `quranic:root_000121:B005/m01`; overstepping a limit `quranic:root_000937:B001/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: 53:14, 53:16 (س د ر); 53:16 (غ ش و); 53:17 (ز ي غ, ب ص ر, ط غ ي).
- Synthesis: The covering is useful pressure on the primary reading: the vision is warranted not by an unobstructed field but by remaining stable under a moving veil.

### 4. Load-Bearing Assembly (P1: 53:1-18)
- Semantic invariant: Strength is produced by tightening, twisting, aligning, and distributing load across prepared materials.
- Surface relation: indirect; 53:5-6, with local lexical anchors through 53:17.
- Surprising reach: The teacher's strength is reframed as engineered tensile integrity rather than undifferentiated force.

#### Subchannel A. Strands are tightened into aligned tensile strength
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Separate strands are tightened into a cord, brought to firmness, and aligned so that the assembly can bear force.
- Active motifs: tightening a knot `quranic:root_000782:B001/m01`; force and solidity `quranic:root_000782:B002/m01`; strength as strands of cord `quranic:root_001274:B001/m01`; tightly twisted cord `quranic:root_001414:B005/m01`; firm strength `quranic:root_001414:B006/m01`; straightening and completion `quranic:root_000766:B002/m01`; settled upright alignment `quranic:root_000766:B003/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: 53:5 (ش د د, ق و ي); 53:6 (م ر ر, س و ي).
- Synthesis: Strength emerges as a relation among tension, twist, and alignment, closely materializing the passage's paired predicates of force and settled form.

#### Subchannel B. Hide, seam, cord, and pad distribute the load
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Hair-on hide is tanned or peeled, sewn into panels, tied by cord, and padded for stable bearing.
- Active motifs: hair-on hide `quranic:root_000844:B006/m01`; hide in tanning `quranic:root_000040:B005/m01`; skin peeling `quranic:root_001267:B002/m01`; sewn hide panels `quranic:root_000121:B006/m01`; tightly twisted cord `quranic:root_001414:B005/m01`; saddle cloth or pad `quranic:root_000766:B010/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: 53:2 (ص ح ب); 53:6 (م ر ر, س و ي); 53:7 (ء ف ق); 53:9 (ق و ب); 53:17 (ب ص ر).
- Synthesis: The network supplies a concrete load-bearing object whose strength depends on preparation and fit, not merely on harder material.

### 5. Names, Shares, and Unwarranted Claims (P2: 53:19-32)
- Semantic invariant: Labels become false claims when inherited naming is used to authorize an unequal distribution without evidence.
- Surface relation: direct; 53:19-23, 53:27-28.
- Surprising reach: Naming and apportionment are one operation: a category label silently predetermines who receives the deficient share.

#### Subchannel A. Inherited labels fabricate stable categories
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: Names received from ancestors are repeated until they appear to designate real, gendered kinds despite lacking authorization.
- Active motifs: naming `quranic:root_000745:B005/m01`; reputation sustained by a name `quranic:root_000745:B008/m01`; father or ancestor `quranic:root_000007:B001/m01`; spoken name `quranic:root_000516:B004/m01`; male category `quranic:root_000516:B001/m01`; female category `quranic:root_000058:B001/m01`; fabricated utterance `quranic:root_001450:B013/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: 53:21 (ذ ك ر, ء ن ث); 53:23, 53:26-27, 53:31 (س م و); 53:23 (ء ب و); 53:24 (م ن ي); 53:29 (ذ ك ر).
- Synthesis: The labels gain social durability from ancestry and repetition, but the channel exposes that durability as manufactured speech rather than ontological proof.

#### Subchannel B. Classification fixes a deficient share
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: A whole is divided into ranked portions; one party reserves the preferred allotment and assigns the diminished part elsewhere.
- Active motifs: division into shares `quranic:root_001226:B003/m01`; third share `quranic:root_000203:B002/m01`; completing a third `quranic:root_000203:B003/m01`; unjust or deficient allocation `quranic:root_000922:B001/m01`; entitlement `quranic:root_000347:B002/m01`; owned right `quranic:root_000347:B003/m01`; legal deed `quranic:root_000516:B008/m01`; wished-for claim `quranic:root_001450:B004/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: 53:20 (ث ل ث); 53:21, 53:29 (ذ ك ر); 53:22 (ق س م, ض ي ز); 53:24 (م ن ي); 53:28 (ح ق ق).
- Synthesis: The unequal division is not incidental to the naming system. The labels act like entries in a deed that assign rights before any proof has been produced.

### 6. Competing Guidance Systems (P2: 53:19-32)
- Semantic invariant: Conduct follows whichever guide is treated as authoritative: evidence and a marked road, or desire and an uncertain trace.
- Surface relation: direct; 53:23, 53:28-30.
- Surprising reach: Guidance is modeled as route-following, animal leadership, shadowing, and choice of a terminal destination.

#### Subchannel A. Desire follows an uncertain trace
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: An inner inclination treats a weak sign as a track, follows it, and mistakes an uncertain outcome for a warranted course.
- Active motifs: tracking a predecessor `quranic:root_000175:B003/m01`; sensory shadow `quranic:root_000175:B008/m01`; weak guess `quranic:root_000969:B003/m01`; uncertain outcome `quranic:root_000969:B006/m01`; desire `quranic:root_001609:B004/m01`; inner intent `quranic:root_001533:B013/m01`; self or essence `quranic:root_001533:B012/m01`; road or course `quranic:root_001583:B002/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: 53:23 (ت ب ع, ظ ن ن, ه و ي, ن ف س, ه د ي); 53:28 (ت ب ع, ظ ن ن); 53:30 (ه د ي); 53:32 (ن ف س).
- Synthesis: Desire does not simply oppose knowledge; it supplies a counterfeit guidance system in which inclination selects the trace and conjecture ratifies the result.

#### Subchannel B. A leader keeps a following body on the road
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A front guide or animal leader moves first while followers, including a calf behind a cow, maintain the same path.
- Active motifs: leader at the front `quranic:root_001583:B003/m01`; gentle road-guidance `quranic:root_001583:B001/m01`; animal leader `quranic:root_001444:B008/m01`; calf following a cow `quranic:root_000175:B007/m01`; consequence following an act `quranic:root_000175:B006/m01`; road `quranic:root_000672:B001/m01`; travelers on a road `quranic:root_000672:B002/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: 53:23, 53:28 (ت ب ع); 53:26-27 (م ل ك); 53:30 (ه د ي, س ب ل).
- Synthesis: Guidance becomes a coordinated procession. The image clarifies both legitimate leadership and the danger that dependent followers inherit the leader's course.

#### Subchannel C. Turning away selects a lower endpoint
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: A person turns the side away from reminder, chooses a near benefit, and stops at the limit of that knowledge.
- Active motifs: turning the side `quranic:root_001001:B005/m01`; turning away `quranic:root_001684:B007/m01`; willing or intending `quranic:root_000610:B001/m01`; reaching a limit `quranic:root_000151:B001/m01`; lower or nearer world `quranic:root_000493:B002/m01`; life as immediate benefit `quranic:root_000383:B013/m01`; spoken reminder `quranic:root_000516:B004/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: 53:21, 53:29 (ذ ك ر); 53:29 (ع ر ض, و ل ي, ر و د, ح ي ي, د ن و); 53:30 (ب ل غ).
- Synthesis: Refusal is rendered as route selection: turning from reminder and preferring the near benefit sets both the direction and the epistemic stopping point.

### 7. Permission-Gated Advocacy (P2: 53:19-32)
- Semantic invariant: Representation is effective only when entry, joining, and acceptance are authorized by the controlling authority.
- Surface relation: direct; 53:25-28, 53:31.
- Surprising reach: Intercession is decomposed into gatekeeping, joinder, approval, and substitution rather than treated as a single magical act.

#### Subchannel A. An advocate enters only through an authorized gate
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: A gatekeeper grants entry, an advocate joins another's case, and the sovereign decides whether that joinder is acceptable.
- Active motifs: permission and gatekeeping `quranic:root_000022:B004/m01`; advocate joining another `quranic:root_000802:B002/m01`; adding or pairing `quranic:root_000802:B001/m01`; acceptance `quranic:root_000569:B001/m01`; winning approval `quranic:root_000569:B004/m01`; sovereign authority `quranic:root_001444:B003/m01`; entity or matter under decision `quranic:root_000831:B001/m01`; sufficing for another `quranic:root_001110:B002/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: 53:26 (ء ذ ن, ش ف ع, ر ض و, م ل ك, ش ي ء, غ ن ي); 53:27 (م ل ك); 53:28 (ش ي ء, غ ن ي).
- Synthesis: The advocate has no independent efficacy. Every transition in the process remains gated by permission and acceptance from the authority that owns the case.

#### Subchannel B. Joining a case does not erase individual liability
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: One party may stand in for or join another, but the account still distinguishes ownership, debt, deed, and due recompense.
- Active motifs: standing in for another `quranic:root_000244:B002/m01`; recompense `quranic:root_000244:B001/m01`; joining as advocate `quranic:root_000802:B002/m01`; owned right `quranic:root_000347:B003/m01`; protected obligation `quranic:root_000347:B007/m01`; legal deed `quranic:root_000516:B008/m01`; wage for work `quranic:root_001046:B004/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: 53:21, 53:29 (ذ ك ر); 53:26 (ش ف ع); 53:28 (ح ق ق); 53:31 (ج ز ي, ع م ل).
- Synthesis: Representation can alter who speaks or stands in the proceeding, but it cannot dissolve the ledger categories by which liability is assigned.

### 8. Action Enters Settlement (P2: 53:19-32)
- Semantic invariant: Conduct becomes an assessable entry that is classified and repaid according to its moral quality.
- Surface relation: direct; 53:31-32.
- Surprising reach: The moral contrast is structured as a work-and-ledger process linking deed, wage, debt, right, and settlement.

#### Subchannel A. Work is classified by its produced effect
- Reading type: surface-primary
- Scene or process: Intentional work produces either harmful or beneficent effects and is entered under the corresponding moral class.
- Active motifs: intentional work `quranic:root_001046:B001/m01`; dealings or practice `quranic:root_001046:B005/m01`; bad or ugly act `quranic:root_000755:B001/m01`; harm produced `quranic:root_000755:B002/m01`; good quality `quranic:root_000323:B001/m01`; beneficent act `quranic:root_000323:B002/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: 53:31 (ع م ل, س و ء, ح س ن).
- Synthesis: Good and bad are not free-floating labels; they describe what intentional conduct becomes and what effects it places into the account.

#### Subchannel B. The ledger converts deeds into due settlement
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: A deed establishes a claim, wage, debt, or protected obligation that is discharged by equivalent recompense.
- Active motifs: legal deed `quranic:root_000516:B008/m01`; wage `quranic:root_001046:B004/m01`; entitlement `quranic:root_000347:B002/m01`; protected obligation `quranic:root_000347:B007/m01`; recompense `quranic:root_000244:B001/m01`; debt settlement `quranic:root_000244:B003/m01`; financial due `quranic:root_000244:B004/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: 53:21, 53:29 (ذ ك ر); 53:28 (ح ق ق); 53:31 (ع م ل, ج ز ي).
- Synthesis: Recompense is activated as account settlement: the quality of work generates a due that must be discharged rather than arbitrarily assigned.

### 9. Moral Contact, Shielding, and Cover (P2: 53:19-32)
- Semantic invariant: Moral safety is maintained by controlling proximity to a boundary and by receiving protective cover after limited contact.
- Surface relation: direct; 53:32.
- Surprising reach: Avoidance, minor contact, piety, and forgiveness form a spatial protection system with distinct preventive and reparative operations.

#### Subchannel A. Avoidance keeps the vulnerable side behind a barrier
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: A person removes the body from dangerous contact, protects the exposed side, and stays behind a barrier before a major breach occurs.
- Active motifs: avoiding or removing `quranic:root_000262:B003/m01`; side as a protected flank `quranic:root_000262:B011/m01`; protective barrier `quranic:root_001677:B001/m01`; self-protection `quranic:root_001677:B002/m01`; piety `quranic:root_001677:B002/m02`; shield `quranic:root_000266:B008/m01`; excessive boundary crossing `quranic:root_001134:B002/m01`; major sin `quranic:root_001281:B007/m01`; minor near-contact `quranic:root_001378:B003/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: 53:32 (ج ن ب, و ق ي, ج ن ن, ف ح ش, ك ب ر, ل م م).
- Synthesis: The distinction between major transgression and minor contact is rendered as distance from a guarded boundary, with piety functioning as active spatial protection.

#### Subchannel B. Forgiveness covers and restores exposed capacity
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: After exposure, a cover is placed over the vulnerable area, making room for recovery, purification, and renewed capacity.
- Active motifs: physical cover `quranic:root_001096:B001/m01`; forgiveness as protection `quranic:root_001096:B002/m01`; expanse `quranic:root_001647:B001/m01`; capacity `quranic:root_001647:B002/m01`; relief after constriction `quranic:root_001647:B005/m01`; purification `quranic:root_000637:B002/m01`; protective barrier `quranic:root_001677:B001/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: 53:32 (غ ف ر, و س ع, ز ك و, و ق ي).
- Synthesis: Forgiveness does more than cancel an entry. It covers what was exposed and restores the person's room to act.

### 10. Material Origins and Hidden Growth (P2: 53:19-32)
- Semantic invariant: Emergence begins inside a concealed enclosure and proceeds through nurture before becoming externally knowable.
- Surface relation: direct; 53:31-32.
- Surprising reach: Human origin in earth and womb is joined by one latent process of enclosure, germination, and emergence.

#### Subchannel A. Formation begins inside a womb-like enclosure
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: An originating center encloses an unseen interior where conception, nurture, and the first emergence of a self occur.
- Active motifs: womb interior `quranic:root_000128:B001/m02`; hidden interior `quranic:root_000128:B002/m01`; mother `quranic:root_000053:B001/m01`; origin or center `quranic:root_000053:B002/m01`; embryo `quranic:root_000266:B007/m01`; conception `quranic:root_001502:B009/m01`; childbirth `quranic:root_001533:B005/m01`; newborn `quranic:root_001533:B005/m03`; developmental nurture `quranic:root_000532:B002/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: 53:23, 53:30, 53:32 (ر ب ب); 53:32 (ب ط ن, ء م م, ج ن ن, ن ش ء, ن ف س).
- Synthesis: Divine knowledge of the self precedes visibility because formation occurs in a protected interior whose stages are hidden from ordinary appraisal.

#### Subchannel B. Soil receives what later rises as living growth
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Fertile ground receives moisture and a concealed beginning, then supports rooted growth and emergence.
- Active motifs: fertile soil `quranic:root_000025:B002/m01`; earth or ground `quranic:root_000025:B001/m01`; rising or emerging `quranic:root_001502:B001/m01`; creation or emergence `quranic:root_001502:B006/m01`; earth revived by rain `quranic:root_000383:B002/m01`; plant growth `quranic:root_000532:B012/m01`; abundant water `quranic:root_001040:B005/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: 53:28, 53:30, 53:32 (ع ل م); 53:29 (ح ي ي); 53:31-32 (ء ر ض); 53:23, 53:30, 53:32 (ر ب ب); 53:32 (ن ش ء).
- Synthesis: The earth-origin statement is activated as germination: what is hidden below becomes a living form through water, rooted support, and staged emergence.

### 11. Water Descends, Gathers, and Sustains (P2: 53:19-32)
- Semantic invariant: Life-supporting water moves from an upper source into lower holding places where it enables settlement and continuity.
- Surface relation: indirect; 53:23, 53:26-32.
- Surprising reach: Sky, road, knowledge, rule, and life roots jointly disclose a compact hydrological process beneath the theological argument.

#### Subchannel A. Rain travels between sky and earth
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A rain-bearing cloud releases water through the intervening space, and dry earth receives it as reviving moisture.
- Active motifs: rain-bearing cloud `quranic:root_000745:B004/m02`; rain between cloud and earth `quranic:root_000672:B005/m01`; earth revived by rain `quranic:root_000383:B002/m01`; cloud `quranic:root_000532:B008/m01`; abundant water `quranic:root_001040:B005/m01`; downward letting `quranic:root_000672:B004/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: 53:23, 53:26-27, 53:31 (س م و); 53:29 (ح ي ي); 53:30 (س ب ل); 53:28, 53:30, 53:32 (ع ل م); 53:23, 53:30, 53:32 (ر ب ب).
- Synthesis: The upper and lower domains are connected by a downward water route, turning the ownership of sky and earth into an ecology of dependence.

#### Subchannel B. Gathered water makes habitation possible
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Water accumulates in a basin or store, sustains breath and life, and permits a population to settle under stable rule.
- Active motifs: basin `quranic:root_000281:B003/m01`; water enabling settlement `quranic:root_001444:B007/m01`; sustaining water `quranic:root_001533:B008/m01`; abundant water `quranic:root_001040:B005/m01`; collected water `quranic:root_000532:B013/m01`; life as usable benefit `quranic:root_000383:B013/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: 53:23 (ج ي ء); 53:26-27 (م ل ك); 53:23, 53:32 (ن ف س); 53:28, 53:30, 53:32 (ع ل م); 53:23, 53:30, 53:32 (ر ب ب); 53:29 (ح ي ي).
- Synthesis: Water is the hidden material condition of settled life: it must be received, retained, and governed before it can sustain a community.

### 12. Work Stopped at a Hard Limit (P3: 53:33-49)
- Semantic invariant: An undertaking can fail by withdrawal, by striking an impenetrable substrate, or by losing the conditions for continued growth.
- Surface relation: direct; 53:33-35.
- Surprising reach: The one who gives little and stops is reframed through excavation and stunted vegetation, two material forms of arrested continuation.

#### Subchannel A. A small gift is followed by deliberate cutoff
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: A giver releases only a little, then closes the hand while repeated requests fail to reopen the flow.
- Active motifs: giving or handing over `quranic:root_001028:B002/m01`; request for a gift `quranic:root_001028:B005/m01`; littleness or scarcity `quranic:root_001251:B001/m01`; small gift followed by withholding `quranic:root_001289:B002/m01`; persistent asking `quranic:root_001289:B006/m01`; tired fingers `quranic:root_001289:B008/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: 53:34 (ع ط و, ق ل ل, ك د ي).
- Synthesis: The stopping point is volitional: limited transfer, closing hand, and unanswered request specify the social mechanics of the withdrawal.

#### Subchannel B. Digging reaches bedrock and cannot continue
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Excavation exposes what was hidden below ground but then strikes a hard substrate that stops further digging.
- Active motifs: bedrock stopping excavation `quranic:root_001289:B001/m01`; hidden tree roots exposed by digging `quranic:root_001117:B008/m01`; directed movement or effort `quranic:root_000709:B001/m01`; work or earning `quranic:root_000709:B002/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: 53:34 (ك د ي); 53:35 (غ ي ب); 53:39-40 (س ع ي).
- Synthesis: The lexical image gives the cutoff a hard physical edge: effort reveals part of the hidden substrate but cannot pass the obstruction.

#### Subchannel C. Growth is stunted when supply fails
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A plant begins to develop but remains small or dries when the sustaining flow is withheld.
- Active motifs: stunted plant `quranic:root_001289:B004/m01`; drought condition `quranic:root_001289:B004/m02`; littleness or diminished state `quranic:root_001251:B001/m01`; rising growth `quranic:root_001502:B001/m01`; plant growth `quranic:root_000532:B012/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: 53:34 (ق ل ل, ك د ي); 53:42, 53:49 (ر ب ب); 53:47 (ن ش ء).
- Synthesis: Withholding is translated into failed development: the interruption affects not only exchange but the future growth that exchange would have sustained.

### 13. Record, Burden, and Full Settlement (P3: 53:33-49)
- Semantic invariant: A transmitted record identifies the responsible bearer, exposes completed effort, and supports settlement without deficit.
- Surface relation: direct; 53:36-41.
- Surprising reach: Scripture, nontransferable burden, visible striving, and recompense operate as stages in one auditable process.

#### Subchannel A. Consequential news is fixed in a readable record
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: A consequential report is delivered, written onto sheets, compiled, and recited in its assigned places.
- Active motifs: consequential news `quranic:root_001464:B002/m01`; divine messenger bearing news `quranic:root_001464:B003/m01`; written sheet `quranic:root_000845:B002/m01`; compiled sheets `quranic:root_000845:B003/m01`; transcription `quranic:root_000845:B005/m01`; misreading `quranic:root_000845:B005/m02`; recitation placed in order `quranic:root_001450:B007/m01`; fulfillment without deficit `quranic:root_001669:B001/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: 53:36 (ن ب ء, ص ح ف); 53:37, 53:41 (و ف ي); 53:46 (م ن ي).
- Synthesis: The prior teaching is treated as an inspectable record whose transmission can be checked for order, completeness, and distortion.

#### Subchannel B. Each bearer retains a distinct load
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: A heavy burden is placed on its own carrier and cannot be shifted to a different bearer or helper.
- Active motifs: physical heavy load `quranic:root_001643:B002/m01`; culpable burden `quranic:root_001643:B002/m02`; helper carrying a chief's burden `quranic:root_001643:B004/m01`; another bearer `quranic:root_000019:B001/m02`; negation of transfer `quranic:root_001390:B001/m01`; lifting or carrying `quranic:root_001251:B004/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: 53:34 (ق ل ل); 53:38 (و ز ر, ء خ ر); 53:39 (ل ي س); 53:47 (ء خ ر).
- Synthesis: Responsibility is materialized as load assignment. Assistance may distribute physical work, but culpability remains attached to the bearer named in the account.

#### Subchannel C. Directed effort is made visible
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: A person's movement toward an end becomes observable work whose extent and direction can be shown.
- Active motifs: directed movement `quranic:root_000709:B001/m01`; work or earning `quranic:root_000709:B002/m01`; ocular seeing `quranic:root_000531:B001/m01`; showing or making visible `quranic:root_000531:B012/m01`; fine perception `quranic:root_000798:B005/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: 53:33, 53:35, 53:40 (ر ء ي); 53:39-40 (س ع ي); 53:49 (ش ع ر).
- Synthesis: The account does not settle on intention alone. Effort becomes a visible trajectory that can be inspected before recompense is assigned.

#### Subchannel D. Settlement is completed without shortfall
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: The full amount due is calculated, matched by equivalent recompense, and delivered without loss.
- Active motifs: completion without loss `quranic:root_001669:B001/m01`; fulfillment of an obligation `quranic:root_001669:B001/m02`; recompense `quranic:root_000244:B001/m01`; debt settlement `quranic:root_000244:B003/m01`; equivalent recompense `quranic:root_001265:B009/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: 53:37, 53:41 (و ف ي); 53:41 (ج ز ي); 53:48 (ق ن ي).
- Synthesis: The emphatic fullness of recompense is a settlement operation: obligation, equivalence, and delivery converge with no remainder withheld.

### 14. Measured Generation and Renewal (P3: 53:33-49)
- Semantic invariant: New life is formed through measured pairing, generative fluid, concealed development, and a later arising.
- Surface relation: direct; 53:44-47.
- Surprising reach: Creation and the later emergence share one staged production process rather than appearing as unrelated assertions.

#### Subchannel A. A paired form is designed to measure
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: A form is measured and embodied as one member of a paired arrangement with differentiated male and female counterparts.
- Active motifs: design or measure `quranic:root_000434:B001/m01`; creation `quranic:root_000434:B002/m01`; embodied form `quranic:root_000434:B003/m01`; paired counterpart `quranic:root_000652:B001/m01`; pair `quranic:root_000652:B003/m01`; male category `quranic:root_000516:B001/m01`; female category `quranic:root_000058:B001/m01`; measure or decree `quranic:root_001450:B001/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: 53:45 (خ ل ق, ز و ج, ذ ك ر, ء ن ث); 53:46 (م ن ي).
- Synthesis: Pairing is a design constraint in the creative operation, with differentiation occurring inside an already measured whole.

#### Subchannel B. Generative fluid initiates concealed development
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: A drop of generative fluid is emitted, enters a conception process, and begins an unseen rise toward formed life.
- Active motifs: generative fluid `quranic:root_001518:B002/m01`; dripping or emission `quranic:root_001518:B003/m01`; semen `quranic:root_001450:B002/m01`; conception `quranic:root_001502:B009/m01`; creation or emergence `quranic:root_001502:B006/m01`; rising `quranic:root_001502:B001/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: 53:46 (ن ط ف, م ن ي); 53:47 (ن ش ء).
- Synthesis: The surface sequence is expanded into a process from emitted material through hidden conception to the first upward movement of a new form.

#### Subchannel C. A later arising renews what had ended
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: After one life course reaches cessation, another emergence raises a new living state.
- Active motifs: subsequent or later state `quranic:root_000019:B001/m01`; rising or emerging `quranic:root_001502:B001/m01`; creation or emergence `quranic:root_001502:B006/m01`; loss of life-force `quranic:root_001454:B001/m01`; reviving life `quranic:root_000383:B002/m01`; creation `quranic:root_000434:B002/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: 53:38, 53:47 (ء خ ر); 53:44 (م و ت, ح ي ي); 53:45 (خ ل ق); 53:47 (ن ش ء).
- Synthesis: The later arising repeats the grammar of initial generation: cessation is followed by another measured emergence into life.

### 15. Life Withdrawn and Revived (P3: 53:33-49)
- Semantic invariant: Life can be removed from an organism or restored to an inert substrate by reversing the flow of animating force.
- Surface relation: direct; 53:43-44, 53:47-49.
- Surprising reach: Death and revival are rendered in both bodily and ecological registers.

#### Subchannel A. Animating force is withdrawn from the body
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: A living creature loses its animating force, reaches destined death, and has its life taken to completion.
- Active motifs: loss of life-force `quranic:root_001454:B001/m01`; stillness of death `quranic:root_001454:B012/m01`; taking the soul `quranic:root_001669:B002/m01`; destined death `quranic:root_001450:B003/m01`; living creature `quranic:root_000383:B003/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: 53:37, 53:41 (و ف ي); 53:44 (م و ت, ح ي ي); 53:46 (م ن ي).
- Synthesis: Death is a completed withdrawal rather than mere disappearance: force ceases, the organism becomes still, and the taking reaches its appointed term.

#### Subchannel B. Water restores life to inert ground
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Dead land receives reviving water, produces vegetation, and rises into a renewed state.
- Active motifs: dead land `quranic:root_001454:B003/m01`; earth revived by rain `quranic:root_000383:B002/m01`; plant growth `quranic:root_000532:B012/m01`; rising growth `quranic:root_001502:B001/m01`; abundant water `quranic:root_001040:B005/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: 53:35 (ع ل م); 53:42, 53:49 (ر ب ب); 53:44 (م و ت, ح ي ي); 53:47 (ن ش ء).
- Synthesis: Revival is materially pictured as rehydration and growth, giving the bodily life-death claim an ecological analogue.

### 16. Outward Signs of Inner Change (P3: 53:33-49)
- Semantic invariant: An inward affect becomes externally legible through an opening face, exposed teeth, tears, and voice.
- Surface relation: direct; 53:43.
- Surprising reach: Laughter and weeping are not just opposites; each is a different bodily disclosure channel.

#### Subchannel A. Laughter opens the face and releases a gleam
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: The face opens, teeth become visible, and brightness or wonder registers the internal shift.
- Active motifs: face opening `quranic:root_000903:B001/m01`; exposed teeth `quranic:root_000903:B001/m02`; joy `quranic:root_000903:B003/m01`; wonder `quranic:root_000903:B003/m02`; gleam `quranic:root_000903:B006/m01`; overflow of affect `quranic:root_000903:B008/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: 53:43 (ض ح ك).
- Synthesis: Laughter is a controlled breach of the face's resting surface through which brightness and affect become visible.

#### Subchannel B. Weeping exits through tears and voiced grief
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Grief crosses the body's boundary as tears and as an audible crying voice.
- Active motifs: inward grief `quranic:root_000146:B001/m01`; tears `quranic:root_000146:B001/m02`; crying voice `quranic:root_000146:B001/m03`; causing weeping `quranic:root_000146:B002/m01`; parallel grief `quranic:root_000147:B001/m01`; parallel tears `quranic:root_000147:B001/m02`; causing a cry `quranic:root_000147:B002/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: 53:43 (ب ك ي).
- Synthesis: Weeping discloses inner condition through both liquid and sound, making the divine causation of affect concretely bodily.

### 17. Provision Held and Accepted (P3: 53:33-49)
- Semantic invariant: Provision becomes stable when it is first possessed and then experienced as sufficient.
- Surface relation: direct; 53:48.
- Surprising reach: Enrichment and contentment are distinct operations: acquisition fills the store, while acceptance settles the recipient.

#### Subchannel A. Wealth is acquired and retained as a store
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: Material value is obtained, kept in possession, and accumulated as a reserve.
- Active motifs: wealth `quranic:root_001110:B001/m01`; acquired or stored property `quranic:root_001265:B002/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: 53:48 (غ ن ي, ق ن ي).
- Synthesis: Enrichment denotes the creation of a durable reserve rather than a momentary transfer.

#### Subchannel B. Sufficiency settles the recipient
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: The received provision reaches adequacy, produces acceptance, and quiets the impulse to seek more.
- Active motifs: sufficiency `quranic:root_001110:B002/m01`; contentment with a grant `quranic:root_001265:B008/m01`; completion without deficit `quranic:root_001669:B001/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: 53:37, 53:41 (و ف ي); 53:48 (غ ن ي, ق ن ي).
- Synthesis: Making sufficient is a change in the recipient's settled state, complementing the external act of increasing what is held.

### 18. Reversal Ends Continuity (P4: 53:50-62)
- Semantic invariant: Destruction reverses an established course until population, movement, and ecological continuity can no longer reproduce themselves.
- Surface relation: direct; 53:50-54.
- Surprising reach: Historical annihilation is materialized through abyss, overturning, covering, drought, and immobilized travel.

#### Subchannel A. A community is reduced to no surviving continuation
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: An established people reaches its terminal outcome, and neither remnant nor capacity to continue is spared.
- Active motifs: destruction or fall `quranic:root_001596:B001/m01`; deadly ravine `quranic:root_001596:B006/m01`; community `quranic:root_001273:B001/m01`; beginning or first state `quranic:root_000067:B001/m01`; outcome or return `quranic:root_000067:B002/m01`; continuation `quranic:root_000142:B001/m01`; remnant `quranic:root_000142:B002/m01`; sparing a remainder `quranic:root_000142:B003/m01`; prior people `quranic:root_001198:B002/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: 53:50, 53:56 (ء و ل); 53:50 (ه ل ك); 53:51 (ب ق ي); 53:52 (ق و م, ق ب ل).
- Synthesis: Annihilation is measured by broken continuity: the first state reaches its outcome and leaves no remainder capable of carrying the community forward.

#### Subchannel B. The settled place is overturned, cast down, and covered
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: A place is diverted from its orientation, thrown into a lower depth, and buried beneath a sweeping cover.
- Active motifs: overturning or diversion `quranic:root_000041:B001/m01`; falling into an abyss `quranic:root_001609:B002/m01`; casting downward `quranic:root_001609:B003/m01`; rapid downward travel `quranic:root_001609:B008/m01`; physical cover `quranic:root_001088:B001/m01`; sweeping cover `quranic:root_001088:B002/m01`; overwhelming punishment `quranic:root_000936:B005/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: 53:53 (ء ف ك, ه و ي); 53:54 (غ ش و); ط غ و is unavailable in `surface_context.root_occurrences` (bundle ayah 53:52).
- Synthesis: The destruction proceeds in ordered physical stages: orientation is reversed, elevation collapses, and the remains disappear under a total cover.

#### Subchannel C. Wind shift and rain failure make the land barren
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Winds change direction, expected rain is withheld, and the damaged place can no longer sustain growth.
- Active motifs: shifting winds `quranic:root_000041:B004/m01`; land deprived of rain `quranic:root_000041:B005/m01`; rainless barren land `quranic:root_001596:B005/m01`; fertilizer or growth input `quranic:root_000739:B005/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: 53:50 (ه ل ك); 53:53 (ء ف ك); 53:61 (س م د).
- Synthesis: Historical destruction extends into ecology: altered atmospheric movement removes the inputs by which the land could recover.

#### Subchannel D. A rapid march ends in immobilization
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A force advances relentlessly until road, mount, and travelers exhaust their capacity and stop.
- Active motifs: road exhausted by travel `quranic:root_001596:B009/m01`; relentless march `quranic:root_000739:B001/m01`; continuity of motion `quranic:root_000739:B009/m01`; fatigue or stopping `quranic:root_001273:B016/m01`; mount breaking down and route stopping `quranic:root_000973:B011/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: 53:50 (ه ل ك); 53:52 (ق و م); 53:61 (س م د); 53:62 (ع ب د).
- Synthesis: Destruction appears as exhausted mobility: even a force defined by advance reaches a point where route and carrier can no longer continue.

### 19. Past Event Becomes Present Warning (P4: 53:50-62)
- Semantic invariant: A prior event acquires present force when it is recalled, retold, and handed to a new audience as an alarm.
- Surface relation: direct; 53:50, 53:52, 53:56, 53:59.
- Surprising reach: Warning is a transmission process that converts historical recurrence into an immediate deictic object.

#### Subchannel A. Repeated precedent forecasts a return
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: An earlier outcome is remembered as a recurring pattern whose return supplies consequential warning.
- Active motifs: return or repetition `quranic:root_001058:B001/m01`; return destination `quranic:root_001058:B002/m01`; habitual recurrence `quranic:root_001058:B004/m01`; earlier or prior state `quranic:root_001198:B002/m01`; alarm or warning `quranic:root_001488:B001/m01`; report `quranic:root_000299:B003/m01`; event becoming a remembered story `quranic:root_000299:B004/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: 53:50 (ع و د); 53:52 (ق ب ل); 53:56 (ن ذ ر); 53:59 (ح د ث).
- Synthesis: The destroyed peoples are not inert history. Repetition converts their outcome into a forecast addressed to the current hearer.

#### Subchannel B. The alarm is handed over as “this”
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A prior report is brought into the speaker's present field, pointed out, and transferred to the hearer as an immediate warning.
- Active motifs: deictic “this” `quranic:root_000527:B003/m01`; taking or handing over `quranic:root_001573:B001/m01`; attention-opening presentation `quranic:root_001573:B002/m01`; alarm or warning `quranic:root_001488:B001/m01`; remembered story `quranic:root_000299:B004/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: 53:56 (ن ذ ر), 53:59 (ح د ث); ه ا ء and ذ و و are unavailable in `surface_context.root_occurrences` (bundle ayahs 53:56, 53:59).
- Synthesis: Deixis performs temporal transfer: the old report is no longer “that” event but an object placed before this audience now.

### 20. Imminence and Uncovering (P4: 53:50-62)
- Semantic invariant: As a concealed event closes distance, only the controlling agent can remove its cover or relieve its pressure.
- Surface relation: direct; 53:57-58.
- Surprising reach: Nearness, exposure, and relief form a single mechanism in which revelation is also the release of constriction.

#### Subchannel A. The interval compresses toward arrival
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: Remaining time and space contract, the event hastens toward its endpoint, and no lower intermediary can divert it.
- Active motifs: temporal nearness `quranic:root_000029:B001/m01`; compressed space `quranic:root_000029:B002/m01`; hastening arrival `quranic:root_000029:B003/m01`; below or short of the goal `quranic:root_000502:B001/m01`; other than the named agent `quranic:root_000502:B003/m01`; categorical negation `quranic:root_001390:B001/m01`; deity as controlling agent `quranic:root_000047:B001/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: 53:57 (ء ز ف); 53:58 (ل ي س, د و ن, ء ل ه); 53:62 (ء ل ه).
- Synthesis: Imminence is spatialized as a narrowing interval with no alternate route of intervention.

#### Subchannel B. Lifting the cover exposes and relieves
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: A cover is lifted, what was hidden becomes visible, and the same act releases the distress created by concealment.
- Active motifs: lifting a cover `quranic:root_001302:B001/m01`; relieving distress `quranic:root_001302:B001/m02`; hidden thing exposed `quranic:root_001302:B002/m01`; watching and waiting `quranic:root_000142:B005/m01`; gleam of exposure `quranic:root_000903:B006/m01`; tooth-like sheen `quranic:root_000967:B005/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: 53:51 (ب ق ي); 53:52 (ظ ل م); 53:58 (ك ش ف); 53:60 (ض ح ك).
- Synthesis: Disclosure and relief are two roles of one uncovering action: the event enters sight at the moment its compressive concealment is removed.

### 21. Audience Posture Reversed (P4: 53:50-62)
- Semantic invariant: A self-protective posture of amused elevation is reversed into bodily lowering before the true master.
- Surface relation: direct; 53:59-62.
- Surprising reach: Wonder, laughter, distraction, standing, prostration, and worship form a continuous bodily response sequence.

#### Subchannel A. Astonishment is deflected into entertainment
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: The audience meets a consequential report with self-regarding wonder, mockery, laughter, and distracting performance.
- Active motifs: wonder `quranic:root_000984:B001/m01`; self-admiration `quranic:root_000984:B002/m01`; object of laughter or mockery `quranic:root_000903:B002/m01`; joy `quranic:root_000903:B003/m01`; wonder through laughter `quranic:root_000903:B003/m02`; distraction through music `quranic:root_000739:B002/m01`; raised head `quranic:root_000739:B004/m01`; proud stance `quranic:root_000739:B004/m02`.
- Ayah anchors: 53:59 (ع ج ب); 53:60 (ض ح ك); 53:61 (س م د).
- Synthesis: The audience converts exposure into spectacle, using sound, laughter, and elevated bearing to avoid the report's demanded response.

#### Subchannel B. Elevated stance yields to full bodily lowering
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: A body moves from upright or head-raised posture to bending, ground contact, and submission.
- Active motifs: standing upright `quranic:root_001273:B002/m01`; raised head `quranic:root_000739:B004/m01`; bodily prostration `quranic:root_000675:B001/m01`; submissive state `quranic:root_000675:B001/m02`; bodily contact points `quranic:root_000675:B003/m01`; bending head and body `quranic:root_000675:B004/m01`; prayer direction `quranic:root_001198:B005/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: 53:52 (ق و م, ق ب ل); 53:61 (س م د); 53:62 (س ج د).
- Synthesis: The command reverses the audience's vertical self-presentation, replacing elevated refusal with a body aligned toward submission.

#### Subchannel C. The servant is correctly aligned under the master
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: Prostration places the servant beneath the rightful master and rejects rival objects that receive the same bodily sign.
- Active motifs: lord or master `quranic:root_000532:B001/m01`; deity `quranic:root_000047:B001/m01`; worship directed to a deity `quranic:root_000047:B001/m02`; servant or slave `quranic:root_000973:B001/m01`; worship `quranic:root_000973:B003/m01`; submission `quranic:root_000973:B003/m02`; image receiving prostration `quranic:root_000675:B006/m01`; king receiving prostration `quranic:root_000675:B006/m02`.
- Ayah anchors: 53:55 (ر ب ب); 53:58, 53:62 (ء ل ه); 53:62 (ع ب د, س ج د).
- Synthesis: The final bodily act resolves the passage's hierarchy: the same posture that can be misdirected is assigned to the master who actually controls disclosure and outcome.

### 22. Binding Speech (P4: 53:50-62)
- Semantic invariant: Speech binds future conduct when utterance is converted into vow, covenant, or secured guarantee.
- Surface relation: indirect; 53:52, 53:55-56.
- Surprising reach: The warning and disputed favors activate a legal-speech channel beneath the closing appeal.

#### Subchannel A. Oath and vow bind the speaker
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A solemn utterance restricts later action by placing the speaker under a declared obligation.
- Active motifs: oath `quranic:root_000048:B003/m01`; vow `quranic:root_001488:B002/m01`; warning utterance `quranic:root_001488:B001/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: 53:55 (ء ل و); 53:56 (ن ذ ر).
- Synthesis: The warning root carries not only alarm but pledged speech, increasing the force of what is announced.

#### Subchannel B. Covenant and guarantee secure performance
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: The obligation is stabilized by covenant and by a guarantee that another future performance will occur.
- Active motifs: covenant `quranic:root_000532:B011/m01`; guarantee `quranic:root_001198:B008/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: 53:52 (ق ب ل); 53:55 (ر ب ب).
- Synthesis: Binding speech matures from declaration into a secured obligation, pressing the audience to treat the warning as enforceable rather than rhetorical.

## Standalone Subchannels

### S1. Gestation approaches visibility (P1: 53:1-18)
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A concealed embryo nears delivery, becomes visibly discernible, and enters the nursing stage.
- Active motifs: embryo `quranic:root_000266:B007/m01`; near delivery `quranic:root_000493:B004/m01`; visible pregnancy `quranic:root_000531:B010/m01`; seminal fluid `quranic:root_001492:B009/m01`; nursing ewe `quranic:root_000532:B009/m02`.
- Ayah anchors: 53:8-9 (د ن و); 53:11-13, 53:18 (ر ء ي); 53:13 (ن ز ل); 53:15 (ج ن ن); 53:18 (ر ب ب).
- Synthesis: The movement from concealment to witnessed manifestation acquires a gestational analogue: nearness is the threshold at which hidden formation becomes visible and support-dependent.

### S2. Failed milk supply interrupts nourishment (P1: 53:1-18)
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Suckling is damaged, milk ceases or cannot be extracted, and the nursing relation fails.
- Active motifs: suckling harmed by failed milk `quranic:root_001116:B003/m01`; milk ceasing `quranic:root_001290:B006/m01`; milk extraction `quranic:root_001416:B001/m01`; nursing ewe `quranic:root_000532:B009/m02`.
- Ayah anchors: 53:2 (غ و ي); 53:11 (ك ذ ب); 53:12 (م ر ي); 53:18 (ر ب ب).
- Synthesis: This compact latent scene pressures denial and dispute through failed nourishment: blockage at the source deprives a dependent receiver.

### S3. Inner appraisal cannot certify itself (P2: 53:19-32)
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: The self forms an inward estimate of its own state, but that estimate remains subordinate to fuller knowledge and tested protection.
- Active motifs: self or essence `quranic:root_001533:B012/m01`; inner intent `quranic:root_001533:B013/m01`; weak guess `quranic:root_000969:B003/m01`; insight through seeing `quranic:root_000531:B001/m02`; purification `quranic:root_000637:B002/m01`; piety `quranic:root_001677:B002/m02`; knowledge that uncovers `quranic:root_001040:B001/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: 53:19 (ر ء ي); 53:23, 53:32 (ن ف س); 53:23, 53:28 (ظ ن ن); 53:28, 53:30, 53:32 (ع ل م); 53:32 (ز ك و, و ق ي).
- Synthesis: Self-purification is epistemically unstable because the evaluator and evaluated object coincide; fuller knowledge must come from outside that loop.

### S4. A penetrating torso wound disrupts breath (P2: 53:19-32)
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A thrust opens a cavity in the side or lung, exposing the body's interior and impairing breath.
- Active motifs: penetrating wound `quranic:root_000347:B009/m01`; gaping wound or empty cavity `quranic:root_001609:B007/m01`; breath `quranic:root_001533:B001/m01`; lung injury `quranic:root_000531:B009/m01`; disease or injury of the side `quranic:root_000262:B007/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: 53:19 (ر ء ي); 53:23 (ه و ي); 53:23, 53:32 (ن ف س); 53:28 (ح ق ق); 53:32 (ج ن ب).
- Synthesis: The channel gives “truth” a sharp, pressure-producing materialization: it penetrates the defended side and exposes an interior on which life depends.

### S5. A palm raceme ripens into held provision (P3: 53:33-49)
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Concealed growth rises through a tree, forms a palm raceme, and ripens into fruit that can be possessed.
- Active motifs: palm raceme `quranic:root_001265:B004/m01`; ripening dates `quranic:root_001684:B016/m01`; rising growth `quranic:root_001502:B001/m01`; plant growth `quranic:root_000532:B012/m01`; acquired or stored provision `quranic:root_001265:B002/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: 53:33 (و ل ي); 53:42, 53:49 (ر ب ب); 53:47 (ن ش ء); 53:48 (ق ن ي).
- Synthesis: Enrichment is materialized as a maturation sequence in which hidden growth becomes a harvest capable of being retained.

### S6. Sirius functions as a precise celestial marker (P3: 53:33-49)
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: A named bright star serves as a conspicuous marker whose recognition requires fine perception.
- Active motifs: Sirius `quranic:root_000798:B008/m01`; conspicuous sign or marker `quranic:root_000798:B006/m01`; fine perception `quranic:root_000798:B005/m01`; landmark or road marker `quranic:root_001040:B002/m01`; lord or master `quranic:root_000532:B001/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: 53:35 (ع ل م); 53:42, 53:49 (ر ب ب); 53:49 (ش ع ر).
- Synthesis: Naming Sirius does not grant it agency; its precision as a marker instead sharpens the contrast between a legible sign and the lord who governs it.

### S7. Blessings are turned into contention (P4: 53:50-62)
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A beneficial grant becomes the object of doubt, sharp dispute, and wrangling rather than recognition.
- Active motifs: blessings or benefits `quranic:root_000048:B004/m01`; sharp debate `quranic:root_001416:B003/m01`; doubt `quranic:root_001416:B004/m01`; wrangling `quranic:root_001609:B009/m01`; wonder `quranic:root_000984:B001/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: 53:53 (ه و ي); 53:55 (ء ل و, م ر ي); 53:59 (ع ج ب).
- Synthesis: The question about favors is activated as a contested-benefit scene: what should elicit recognition is instead converted into argumentative resistance.

### S8. Racehorses are aligned, released, and checked
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: An unridden horse is led to a starting cord, aligned with the field, released by lengthening the rein, and tested for straightness, stride, hoof placement, and sound footing.
- Active motifs: race-start cord `quranic:root_001269:B006/m01`; line-up point `quranic:root_001269:B006/m02`; leading an unridden horse `quranic:root_001519:B005/m01`; extending the rein to increase speed `quranic:root_000151:B007/m01`; broad rapid stride `quranic:root_001647:B006/m01`; matching hind and fore hoofprints `quranic:root_000347:B014/m01`; sideways running that resists straightening `quranic:root_001001:B012/m01`; protecting a painful hoof `quranic:root_001677:B003/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: 53:3 (ن ط ق); 53:9 (ق و س); 53:28 (ح ق ق); 53:29 (ع ر ض); 53:30 (ب ل غ); 53:32 (و س ع, و ق ي).
- Synthesis: This materializes the surah's measured course as race discipline: alignment precedes speed, straight running is distinguishable from lateral deviation, and even strong forward motion remains accountable to precise footing.

### S9. An intact-looking eye can misregister what it sees
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: A visible pupil remains in place while a roving defect or woven film degrades perception from distortion, through double vision, to an eye whose globe remains intact after sight is gone.
- Active motifs: reflected human image in the pupil `quranic:root_000059:B005/m01`; roving eye defect `quranic:root_000610:B007/m01`; woven film over the eye `quranic:root_000672:B010/m01`; seeing one object as two `quranic:root_000802:B006/m01`; intact visible eye globe `quranic:root_001273:B021/m01`; lost sight despite intact appearance `quranic:root_001273:B021/m02`.
- Ayah anchors: 53:24, 53:39 (ء ن س); 53:26 (ش ف ع); 53:29 (ر و د); 53:30 (س ب ل); 53:52 (ق و م).
- Synthesis: This usefully pressures the witness language of 53:11-18: the mere presence of an eye or image does not guarantee a faithful report, so the insistence that sight neither drifted nor exceeded its bound becomes a concrete reliability condition.

### S10. Marked gaming arrows allocate shares by chance
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A set of gaming arrows is kept in a leather holder, including a specifically ranked fifth arrow, and used in a procedure that divides portions and can assign a third share.
- Active motifs: leather holder for gaming arrows `quranic:root_000532:B010/m01`; collected set of lots `quranic:root_000532:B010/m02`; fifth maysir arrow `quranic:root_001533:B016/m01`; division into shares `quranic:root_001226:B003/m01`; third portion `quranic:root_000203:B002/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: 53:18, 53:23, 53:30, 53:32, 53:42, 53:49, 53:55 (ر ب ب); 53:20 (ث ل ث); 53:22 (ق س م); 53:23, 53:32 (ن ف س).
- Synthesis: This reframes the disputed apportionment at 53:19-28 as a chance-allocation apparatus: a named lot can produce a share, but neither its label nor its desired outcome supplies the proof that the passage demands.

### S11. A hunting party must verify the kill
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Hunters set out, prepare a trapping pit, pursue quarry that runs and then stops to look behind, and finally inspect the struck animal to determine whether it has actually died.
- Active motifs: setting out to hunt `quranic:root_000745:B006/m01`; hunter's trapping pit `quranic:root_001116:B004/m01`; quarry running a course `quranic:root_001290:B007/m01`; quarry stopping to look back `quranic:root_001290:B007/m02`; inspecting whether the quarry died `quranic:root_001454:B014/m01`; confirmed death `quranic:root_001454:B014/m02`.
- Ayah anchors: 53:2 (غ و ي); 53:11 (ك ذ ب); 53:23, 53:26-27, 53:31 (س م و); 53:44 (م و ت).
- Synthesis: This materializes the surah's contrast between conjecture and warranted perception: movement, disappearance, or a successful strike is not enough, because the outcome still has to be observed and verified.

### S12. Marriage joins pair, contract, and escorted transition
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: Two people stand in a marital pair, a marriage contract establishes the relation, and the bride is escorted to her spouse as the contract takes social effect.
- Active motifs: marital spouse relation `quranic:root_000652:B002/m01`; marriage contract `quranic:root_001444:B004/m01`; bride as participant `quranic:root_001583:B006/m01`; escorting the bride to her spouse `quranic:root_001583:B006/m02`.
- Ayah anchors: 53:23, 53:30 (ه د ي); 53:26-27 (م ل ك); 53:45 (ز و ج).
- Synthesis: This supports the creation of pairs at 53:45 by materializing pairing as an enacted relation, while usefully pressuring the mere assignment of gendered names at 53:27: a label alone does not perform the relation that contract and transition establish.

### S13. A slave earns the price of release
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: A person held in slavery performs remunerated work toward the price of the neck, settles the remaining due, gains release, and enters the legal patronage created by manumission.
- Active motifs: enslavement `quranic:root_000973:B004/m01`; labor toward one's own price `quranic:root_000709:B005/m01`; unpaid balance of the redemption price `quranic:root_000709:B005/m02`; release when the price is met `quranic:root_000709:B005/m03`; debt settlement `quranic:root_000244:B003/m01`; patronage after manumission `quranic:root_001684:B005/m02`.
- Ayah anchors: 53:10, 53:62 (ع ب د); 53:29, 53:33 (و ل ي); 53:31, 53:41 (ج ز ي); 53:39-40 (س ع ي).
- Synthesis: This materializes 53:39-41 as a status-changing settlement: the person's own striving generates the payment, and full discharge of the due produces release rather than a transferable reward.

### S14. The dead are shrouded, carried, and hidden in a grave
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: The dead body is covered in a shroud, raised on a bier, carried to a grave, and placed below sight.
- Active motifs: bier or funeral carrier `quranic:root_000067:B008/m03`; object carried aloft `quranic:root_001042:B008/m01`; covering the dead `quranic:root_000266:B009/m01`; shroud `quranic:root_000266:B009/m02`; grave `quranic:root_000266:B009/m03`; burial below sight `quranic:root_001117:B009/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: 53:7 (ع ل و); 53:15, 53:32 (ج ن ن); 53:25, 53:50, 53:56 (ء و ل); 53:35 (غ ي ب).
- Synthesis: This materializes the interval between causing death at 53:44 and producing the later arising at 53:47: burial is a deliberate concealment of the body, so renewed emergence answers a concrete passage below sight rather than an abstract cessation.

### S15. A drink is reduced, rested, and taken by measured sips
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Fire heats a covered cooking place until two-thirds of a drink are gone; the remaining third is transferred to a large vessel, rested until it improves, and consumed in paced sips.
- Active motifs: cooking fire and heat `quranic:root_001122:B001/m01`; oven cover `quranic:root_001669:B005/m01`; drink reduced until one third remains `quranic:root_000203:B008/m01`; storage vessel `quranic:root_000067:B010/m01`; resting the drink for days `quranic:root_000067:B010/m02`; improved drink after resting `quranic:root_000067:B010/m03`; large jar `quranic:root_001251:B003/m01`; sip `quranic:root_001533:B006/m01`; pause between sips `quranic:root_001533:B006/m02`.
- Ayah anchors: 53:11 (ف ء د); 53:20 (ث ل ث); 53:23, 53:32 (ن ف س); 53:25, 53:50, 53:56 (ء و ل); 53:34 (ق ل ل); 53:37, 53:41 (و ف ي).
- Synthesis: This usefully pressures the surah's disputed “third” by making a third an exact remainder produced through observable loss and controlled handling, unlike the self-serving share assignment at 53:21-22.

### S16. A well-made cloth can lie by its appearance
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: Cloth is tightly woven, given its first fold, dyed or ornamented so that its appearance misstates its condition, and displayed for inspection or sale.
- Active motifs: tightly woven cloth `quranic:root_000347:B010/m01`; first fold of a cloth `quranic:root_001226:B007/m01`; dyed or ornamented cloth `quranic:root_001290:B009/m01`; cloth that lies by its condition `quranic:root_001290:B009/m02`; merchandise displayed for inspection `quranic:root_001001:B002/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: 53:11 (ك ذ ب); 53:22 (ق س م); 53:28 (ح ق ق); 53:29 (ع ر ض).
- Synthesis: This usefully pressures the claim that the heart did not belie what it saw: a polished surface can itself “lie,” so reliable witnessing requires correspondence between appearance and underlying condition, not visual attractiveness alone.

### S17. Supplication joins invocation, “amen,” and tears
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: A worshiper invokes the divine name, asks for response by saying “amen,” and gives the plea bodily force through tears and a voiced cry.
- Active motifs: divine name in invocation `quranic:root_000047:B002/m01`; saying “amen” `quranic:root_000054:B003/m01`; request for response `quranic:root_000054:B003/m02`; tears `quranic:root_000147:B001/m02`; voiced weeping `quranic:root_000147:B001/m03`.
- Ayah anchors: 53:23, 53:25-26, 53:31, 53:58, 53:62 (ء ل ه); 53:27 (ء م ن); 53:43, 53:60 (ب ك ي).
- Synthesis: This supports the closing call to worship and the rebuke for laughing rather than weeping by reframing tears as directed supplication: affect becomes speech seeking an answer rather than an undirected display.

### S18. A vow is discharged through sanctuary offering and worship
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: A person assumes a vow, dedicates and sends an offering to the sanctuary, and completes the act at a prayer place through worship.
- Active motifs: self-imposed vow `quranic:root_001488:B002/m01`; sanctuary offering `quranic:root_001583:B005/m01`; sending the offering `quranic:root_001583:B005/m02`; prayer place `quranic:root_000675:B002/m01`; worship `quranic:root_000973:B003/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: 53:10, 53:62 (ع ب د); 53:23, 53:30 (ه د ي); 53:56 (ن ذ ر); 53:62 (س ج د).
- Synthesis: This materializes the final command to worship as an enacted obligation involving declared commitment, dedicated property, movement toward a sacred destination, and bodily completion.

### S19. Armed opponents descend into a duel
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Opponents prepare shield and war gear, descend together to the fighting ground, stand against one another, and exchange a sword blow.
